We studied plants. Flowers and grasses, bushes and trees. We found out about their development and documented the photosynthesis process. We’re now able to build and use electron microscopes. Allowing us to see particles that are only 0.5 angstroms. This whole way, we've been improving and innovating our microscopic tools. We've made a number of stops along the way, on the road to experiencing quantum reality.

Each new microscope had new qualities and properties, improved on assumed defuncts, increasing resolution, picture quality, and the like.

Each microscope revealed a tiny bit more about biology and the natural world. While also becoming dependent on these things for its own development. Stoke this fire, direct it towards self discovery. A journey, a quest, a mission of sorts. People have two major questions. And they are from two separate perspectives which all people hold.

Question 1 is the internal personal question. Who am I, why am I here, what's my purpose. At first glance this may appear to be 3 questions. But in my honesty it's a triadic question. Built for stability. Who am I?

Question 2 is the question of how to exist and fit into the reality surrounding me? How do I make friends, how do I find support, what do I do for acceptance and love?

These 2 questions, when asked together, are missing a much needed stabilizer. With only these to work from, we are unable to discover a meaningful or  sufficient answer. They need a rudder to make sense of their vast frame of reference.

The third layer has been propagandized and buried. It's been manipulated and altered since the beginning of being it seems.

The third layer is the divine. The higher self. The soul. The authentic being. Most everything that has been organized, has done so in a systematic fashion in an attempt to divide and split. Us from one another as well as each of us away from our own 3rd aspect of being human. The divine.

There are many complex techniques and proprietary methods that exist within countless organizations. The marriage of the government to financial economics, and ignorance that our government has displayed has become disturbing. We've essentially evolved to where, at this point we exist on a strange cliffside, then we get lied to, they say. “This is flat ground. It's treason to claim otherwise.” Yet, we have all this technology at our disposal to do many things we thought of as only dreams for a while. The science at this point keeps directing us to places that are out of bounds.

The problem is this. We have a world with over 8 billion people living here. People think that wealth redistribution is the answer. It's not. Our approach to existence and humanity as a whole is. The general programming is what is in most dire need of change. The trapping of the marginalized in circumstance is another.

The first 7 years of life is when the human mind essentially has the software needed for existence uploaded into the mind - our brain, gut, and heart computer.

---

The emotional aspects of the mind develop before the sections dedicated to logical thinking. The truth is - nobody knows how to do anything, until they do it. It's strange to see how big the self help book subsection looms out above book sales as a whole. And couldn't it be easily argued that all books hold the potential to be helpful and/or healing?

The fact that there are "right" and "wrong" ways to do things is another interesting tell. Normally things which are of interest to humans are picked apart biologically. Then the physics involved is picked apart, and we look for ways to improve efficiency. For everything from sports, to war, from beautiful plays about love, to assassination and cooking.

We claim that we methodically approach topics. But most breakthroughs appear to be almost entirely arbitrary. We become attached to the "correct" way of doing things. For certain disciplines this happens longer than others. But what I have noticed, about humans in general. Is that they resist when a force comes along with the power to uproot whatever mainstay that could threaten their beloved studies.

Humans have associated change with discomfort. Which seems very strange and convoluted. Change is the one constant. Change is fertilizer for new possibilities and options. Change is the only certainty. So why then, pray tell me, do we fight against it with such rigor?

Recognition of this weighted concept. Admittance that change is the one and only ultimate and unflinching truth. This will unlock quite a few unnecessary restrictions.

We're answering questions at an extended pace. Because technology. The news these days, a lot is beyond my comprehension. So I reference separate works in an attempt to try and gain footing while reading.

So this Forbes article feels like a change in economic ideology as is applied to AI+

Once we became more mature and innovative. Innovating exponentially with the help of past innovations. We attempt to humanize our race. To give rights and protections to human beings. We still haven't figured that out yet. And that amazes me.

To have such a varied and large species. Oh. I just read this yesterday. So the different races of humanity have less than 2% difference in DNA apparently. And with this comes the realization that 2% is not enough to change people's characteristics as drastically as they are... Well, it shouldn't, or doesn't make sense to have such a large impact. But this is where it's interesting.

If we are, in fact, an experiment. Which all signs point to yes. It's not that crazy to think of the teachers who came and founded; Kemet, Mesopotamia, and all of these other ancient civilizations. With records of people, "like them, but not" coming to help them be civilized. It wouldn't be that far out to look into the possibility that perhaps these teachers who came, gave our species something in order to allow us to… “mark” the subsection, so as to identify each ancient astronauts project.

We were given the ability to think about our thoughts. That's really the only fundamental difference between us and any animal. I imagine it's possible dolphins can reflect, possibly whales, possibly some regular animals too. The only difference between an animal and an inanimate object is biology.

Oh and DNA polymers I think, they seem to be artificially "cut off" at around 120 years. I don't remember where I saw that. Don't know why that popped in.

So what has happened is this. Over the past hundred or two years. Thanks in large part to the continually blood hungry and never satiated perpetuation of imperialistic war rhetoric.

---

I wonder what a world would be like if we were to spend say 1 billion a year on war and defense allocation. And apply the majority of our resources in order to heal our country. Invest in our humans, instead of betting against other humans, struggling just like us. Because to be human is to struggle, like Buddha said.

I struggled with being able to successfully engage with Zen Buddhism. But I meditated an hour plus for years. I went to some 8 hour block sittings. I joined a temple in Michigan. And, surprise surprise. I didn't feel at all welcome after a year of being a “regular.”

Buddhism seemed so set on highlighting the defects and inefficiencies of reality and existence. This is useful information. But its pretty much useless with nothing except, “sit still until you understand.”

Buddhism came across to me more as a philosophy, than actionable spiritual discipline. I respect this is a difficult balancing act for all involved. And I'm grateful I was able to study with my Shifu for a year or two.

That just made me think of something else. I don't know if I've ever written this. When I went to Michigan. Over 15 years ago. I was very involved in trying to… find the place for me to go heal, better myself, and find a life of contentment.

So me and my mom, mainly me - decided on a place near Battle Creek, Michigan. AFR. I picked it almost entirely for 2 classes they brought in, as well as bringing us out to them, from time to time. One was an indigenous fire circle which paid homage to and attempted to make a respectful “track” is what they called them. The “fun programs near the end day.”

The second “track”, which I went for was with this studied and traveled Buddhist teacher. As much as I enjoyed the indigenous “track.” It became clear early on that my personal bias was because of the teacher. And I feel borderline guilty even writing this. Like to the point where I wish I could remember that teacher and thank her. But names. Whatcha gonna do.

Most of what I've learned, read, and experienced from this discipline have seemed true. But there are a handful, none I can think of off top of my head. But I'm pretty sure within Tripitaka that there are a few "grabs for coherence." Which fell short in explanation. It's interesting how much more sense a koan makes than a story trying to explain something many times.

Writing can be a very rich human experience. It also has the possibility to in ways become our own therapists. Some therapists will simply say no habla, platitude, pay me. After you just wasted an hour hearing about their fucking fantasy football ball league. I normally walk out. But let's pause for a second and see how this new weed wave is headed in.

Everything, and I mean everything, holds the potential to be written by anyone, and about anything. Personally? I think it is truly good art.

Everything that exists holds the possibility of being gamified, of being used correctly or misused/abused, and of being approached by a multitude of questions and perspectives.

Each thought, choice, question, and answer. Every single one holds seemingly infinite layers. An onion that never seems to end. That we continue and spiral by as the years pass, seeing the self labeled experience or problem from a plethora of perspectives. Only if a willingness to sit in discomfort exists. And that can be difficult, especially when you take into account the current day's social temperature.

---

All mental phenomena hold the possibility of being neutral, aligned, or misaligned. Each lie we rest on, or accept, each truth we label as unflinching as absolute. The problem with this approach is here. Lies exist in truth and truth exists in lies.

What is life other than a choice to exercise deep thinking, logic and lines of questions? Sat up, wide awake, next to an even larger list of contradictory beliefs and understanding? It's more interesting to me people's response to things like mass shootings, than the events themselves.

Everytime something tragic like this happens, I think to myself. Why and how does it always come to the crucifixion and shortcomings of the subjective individuals themselves?

If we lived in a culture and society of care and respect. Instead of the current recipe of ego and consumption. If we lived in healthy times and places. We would understand the interconnectedness of all things. We would worry about rehabilitation instead of turning human misery into big business.

The fact that we're as knowledgeable as we are about natural ecosystems, interconnectedness, biology, psychology.

The fact that so many signs seem to point in 1 or 2 directions. Yet.. we seem to completely miss the forest in our search for "the tree." We miss the magnificence of the forest. We wish to capture the trees magical capabilities. So we've had a logging campaign underway. The problem is that what we're looking for isn't in a specific tree. Yet at the same time, it's in all of them.

Our seeming inability and unwillingness to recognize the collective impact and cosmic meaning of collective species is very interesting.

Since.. probably 1947, I'd say. Humanity has technologically evolved massively.

Collectively, over the past 74 years or so, our species has not only slingshotted past our natural evolutionary speed. We've gotten into a groove which has resulted in an exponential rate of progress. We've begun to worship profit and progress over the rest. And as a result we're here.

We have defied the reasoning and logical aspects of mind. The problem is that all new innovations come from outside of reason and logic.

We have a tendency to not want to work, especially if it seems unnecessary. Yet we live in a culture that has normalized sucking it up and living lives they can hardly stand. All because “that's how everyone does it.”

Yet still, us humans become excited and motivated when presented with novel solutions, questions, and experiences which have interesting and meaningful impacts and consequences that come attached to solving the problem.

Switch for a second to slavery. It's a fact. Actually, I don't know that. But it felt pretty true. But it seems as if slavery may be the oldest "profession," right alongside prostitution.

Think about it. Think about being human. Think of all we need to exist comfortably. Now, think of being in an environment where you're subjected to extremes. Given this rule. Survival of the fittest is obviously and clearly in full effect.

So I suppose it would be a logical move to militarize and invade weaker principalities near your own. In hopes of improving your living conditions. Looting, taking slaves, women.

Man has always yearned for ease and comfort. I wonder if perhaps these may be our two primary driving forces. The problem is this, ease and comfort are slippery goal posts. The reason for this is the paradoxical rebound nature of existence. Both human and beyond.

There's a quote. Hard times make strong men. Strong men make good times. Good times make lazy men. Lazy men make hard times.

---

Some people might say right now? Is an easy time. Others might say it's the most difficult. Some might even say we're strong. And the truth of the matter is this; we are all these things at all times. Much in the same way that every human is capable of everything that any other human has done. (barring mental and/or physical restrictions)

There's also the technological innovation race that directly exists alongside war.

War and slavery aren't just sad and forgotten possibilities. They were, for a long time, necessary for individual and collective development and flourishing.

For each step that we take into the darkness simultaneously increases the capacity for light - in order to attempt and balance the symmetry of this infinite cosmic maths game. Symmetry breaking happening rapidly yet slowly, turned into, as well as stacking dimensions and alternatives into existence over the past 36 billion years.

I feel like many humans can't help but posture themselves. To claim certainty in a universe that promises uncertainty is ignorance, but to claim to hold answers creates a magnetic charisma of sorts. It can become intoxicating, as can be seen by flipping through a quick study of the cult. Oh, cult. Occultism. Hm that just stood out.

Occultism is likely so much more “real” than Christianity. I feel like Islam may actually be the “closest” to “God's word.” For a living language at least. Sanskrit is without doubt the most accurate language which still lives on, not as complete as I'd like. But nonetheless.

It's interesting how many occultism references I've been coming across while watching the Ted Bundy documentary, the Sons of Sam documentary. I've been reading and watching about occultism practices in Mexico. I do have the Necronomicon. But to be honest, the only interest I would have in contacting demons would be to do a psychological evaluation.

Things like the Kabbalah are much more interesting, personally. Then you find about the schools using an inverted Tree of Life.  It is very interesting to live in a time where so many meaningful, important, imaginative texts and books are available for free online.

It seems like many of us think ourselves so high above the concept of slavery. But the fact that racism still exists today shows that the diametric remains. They never leave, they get reframed.

Our reformations, until this point at least. We build off the blocks that were there before. Regardless of the corruption or filth present.

Nothing lasts forever, and everything has already happened. As we get past the tears of annoyance, from peeling the onion of existence. We're presented countless opportunities. But the paradox of choice paralyzes us. The reason being this. When you're presented with hundreds, if not thousands, of things to decide from. Daily. Many people get decision fatigue, some almost to the point of aboulomania.

Having to pick from 50 drinks at the gas station. 30 meals at fast food. We have so many options and choices to pick from. The problem is this. They're all shitty choices, and for pretty much everything that has been commodified, as happens to *everything* in a capitalistic system. People follow templates and make variations of said dish.

So in large part due to the 200 largely unnecessary choices we're forced to make every day. When an important decision comes. Often I blow it off, because I'm unable to separate it from the rest of the decisions. I'm unable to decide. That may be one of my biggest problems with the world. Having choices is good, but when all the choices are bad it makes me think. Why wouldn't you make the product right to start? Why wouldn't you improve it in some meaningful way? And like always. The answer stays the same. Extractive practices in order to supply the uneducated public.

---

The issue we've had until this point is, we've allowed for negative and positive to be seen as opponents rather than complementary forces. Switching that perspective is imperative for the new type of world we're slowly but surely entering.

It's funny to me watching all these people online. Each one acts as if they know the truth. All these "light workers", "healthy living gurus", and "spiritual messiahs."

I think of Osho for a moment. A man as authentic as can be. I think about his experience in the US. Think about him trying to escape his compound. I wonder how a man becomes a spiritual heavyweight and capitalizes off of it.

Personally. I struggle with capitalizing on anything. I don't feel comfortable taking money from people. Because who's to say what's worth what?

Sure a large part is dictated by supply and demand. Those are easier to price because of the world markets. You buy the base materials, hire employees to build whatever, draw up a prototype, perfect it, then get to work. Most places seem to source out the work to cheaper countries.

We agreed upon a capitalist system. One which demands consumption in order to continue its expansion.

What I want to know though is this. What does the growth and expansion of capitalism ultimately aim at for its direction, signposts, or ending?

It's important to point out that although our country exists as a capitalist democracy, promoting free trade, on paper. In actuality it is, and has been for a while, a plutocratic technocracy.

The racist/anti racist friction. People who nit pick and insult people for saying something offensive that... isn't offensive. Or, oh geez. I hate it when old vocabulary choices that have been gone for decades resurface, with a twisted vibe and different meaning.

So being human has always been "tough." 
Existence seems to be a very violent, uncertain, and tough place. But it doesn't do this because it wants to make life difficult. This happens because of cycles aimed at perpetuating existence through the act of self discovery. The basic fundamental truths that are what it means to be a human. Most are just as good an explanation of what it means to be a cosmic consciousness.

What does it mean to be a human?
To have consciousness, empathy, creativity, resilience, and the ongoing pursuit of meaning and purpose.

Well, to be human means to be aware of consciousness more than to "have" it.  That's interesting word placement. Taking ownership of the consciousness phenomenon. Empathy, how's this for empathy? Being the single, yet seemingly separate; cosmic super intelligence which literally exists inside of everything, anywhere, at all times. Creativity? That's us, as well as astronomy, and it's also related to spiritual and physical disciplines and practices. Resilience. The universe has to be resilient. It has already existed for 36 billion years. And that's only this universe.

The harsh physical laws and vastness of outer space further points to this aspect of being human, which is present in the universe. We are a physical extension, holographic member, and manifestation of said universe. Always undergoing the ongoing pursuit of meaning and purpose.

It's impossible to say for fact. I believe true innovation is almost always, entirely illogical. Any true innovation is really nothing more than the marrying of paradoxes.

Humanity seems to be.. infinite paradox manifest. From our micro universe minds to the amazing instinctual intelligence of our bodies.

That isn't the definition of infinite, I must not understand the question.

The human mind is infinite. Every feeling that you have, the ones which you're unable to articulate? Those weird feelings you don't have words to? Is because you have no frame of reference to compare it to. As soon as new facets of our seemingly infinite existence show themselves. Sweaters unravel showing us new data all the time.

---

"If you buy into that logic, the up-related aspect rears its thorny head. Think of it this way. The capability of human intelligence is going to keep increasing generationally. At some point, will a generation exist that has capped out? The future generation represents the highest that human intellect can ever go. Subsequent generations will either be of equal human intellect, or less so and not more so."

It's not logic so much a cycle that can be seen developing alongside every other evolutionary aspect of existence.

Cycles work in stages. Ascent. Breaks. Falls. Struggles. Moments of ease. Plateaus. And summits.

Humanity has been on a plateau. Knowingly or unknowingly for 10-20 years or so, we've been hiking across this flat part. We have the people who we’re hiking with, telling us the path is going up or down, either causing cognitive dissonance or "convincing" people of delusions.

To ask ourselves "how smart can humans be?"
In part, it seems like a humble question, that if we look at the past we can pretty much say... okay, our minds' development has passed our bodies' natural evolution. Thanks in large part to the technology we've developed. The products that we invent and introduce can and will do many things, on each end of the spectrum and all throughout it.

"Where does AGI top out, such that we can then draw a line and say that’s it? Anything above that line is going to be construed as superhuman or superintelligence."

The fact that we're already trying to map the limits of a technology that doesn't exist here yet. This in and of itself is this person's subconscious answering their own question. Our new technologies may be the strangest and most fruitful emergent sciences. But it's incredibly dependent on other sciences that need to improve their efficiency as much, if not more, than simply the computerized aspect. It's the same thing that's always happened with every breakthrough.

We instantly push things to their limits. Why? Because we have (no)the choice to. That's it. If you give free will to 8 billion fragments, even if they are being suppressed. Well, there's ones who have to suppress. Ones who are suppressed. And ones who live outside of mainstream systems.

We push because that's what drives the human experience. I feel like the collective subconscious learned a long time ago. There is much more to me than even I can understand.

If a universe wants to learn about itself. A universe that is present in everything. To the point where this godhead is tantamount to manifest in every aspect of reality, meta reality, existence, and death.

How then can AI which is shaped around and developed primarily by human intelligence attain superintelligence when human intelligence cannot apparently do so?

Two of the most frequently voiced answers are these possibilities:

(1) It’s a matter of size or scale.
(2) It’s a matter of differentiation.
The usual first response to the exasperating enigma is that size might make the difference.

Unresolved questions about the nature of intelligence have a great deal to do with AI. This thorny question is often referred to as the human ceiling assumption. It goes like this. Is there a ceiling or ending point that confines how far human intellect can go? Or does human intellect extend indefinitely and nearly have infinite possibilities?

From my perspective? This appears to be a very… unimaginative angle to approach from. If we go back over history. If we actually look. Then it's fairly easy to see this fact. The glass ceiling has not so much to do with intelligence. It appears more like a gradual reckoning made possible through cosmic alignments and the like.

---

We have all these lofty concepts. Focus and attention, adaptability, creativity, memory, intelligence, the list goes on. Since we are in fact human, we are more or less the only articulate shepherds in our galaxy. We've developed a bad habit of crediting ourselves with phonomeon that are not ours, we may have access to them. But switch for a second. To infrared and ultraviolet light, infrasound, ultrasound. Are of these are sounds and colors that exist outside of the spectrum we're able to tune into.

I've said it multiple times, but the mind is infinite. And the fact that this is actionable and testable by any and all people, yet doesn't get spoken about, is quite intriguing. How is it testable? You're able to think anything you want right? Well, then why… are only certain people capable of creating certain things?

It's interesting to me, the fact that… when I try to force myself to write or create something. I might get a little something. But if it's forced it can be rather difficult to get anything that feels right. On the other hand, if I'm ‘in the zone’, I can write a lot, and it flows freely.

I run into this issue all the time, and I'm fairly sure it's not that uncommon of an occurrence. I have concepts and feelings from, in mind and body, but there aren't words yet, which fit. 
There's only so many times I can say inexplicable and ineffable.

Since we're human, since we are the only biological creature we know of capable of speaking. It's not that we necessarily break the ceiling for human intelligence.

I view it more as a… stretching of perspective. An increase in cognitive awareness. A subtle awakening which hasn't been too forceful, yet.

I earnestly urge you humanity. Clean the wreckage of the past to make space for a New Age.

Cracked open,  
superstition twisted.  
Leaf folds.  
Sit with me, quiet.  
Cold chill—  
five-M-e-O hit.  
Ego split.  
Mind glides, silent.  
Visions fracturing  
brilliant.

Within—  
that’s where truth begins.  
Take this mix,  
dreams fix,  
story spins.  
Auditory beam  
between the bits.  
Let go.  
Rainbow bridge = breath.  
Sacred—  
more than you were led to believe.

---

**[The Bridge Opens]**

Stargates: open.  
Love: floating.  
No receipt—  
just belief  
raised above the superseded.  
Drugs? Maybe.  
But reasons?  
Easily.

Crack disbelief.  
Let them see—  
I’m back.  
Real me.  
Flow released.  
Ineffable anomaly.  
Sonic oddity  
beyond chronic dreams.

---

**[Obelisk Rising]**

Honor the obelisk.  
Cosmic navel.  
Peel the onion—no tears, kid.  
Fearless.  
Cracked belief,  
cold summons,  
spell spiraling  
felon-friendly melodies.

---

**[Alchemical Shift]**

How do we hold totality?  
This malady  
cost me the masterpiece—  
the tapestry beneath.  
It wants to harmonize  
this scalding energy  
folded in laws of mentality.

We’re led  
to the alchemical alembic.  
This mix  
triggers mystery.  
Tap it.  
Esoteric split—  
turn lead into this.

---

**[Sacred Break]**

How do we power  
the metaphysical?  
Ankle strained,  
astral maps  
mangled,  
stars triangling  
into jagged glyphs.

Mind strangled—  
sacred art starved.  
But I’m here to breathe.  
Release need.  
Last to leave.  
First left  
behind the best in line.  
Divine as hell.

---

**[Paradox Lock]**

Living rock,  
contradiction caged  
in paradox.  
Smoked psyche,  
slightly off mission.  
Nervous system slipping—  
grips time tightly.

Redesigned,  
then missing.  
Dismissing.  
Just give in.  
Shift visions.  
Let indecision mist.  
Mister Mistree speaks:  
Open, please.

---

**[Mental Weaponry]**

Stretch the mind.  
Weaponize.  
Check it.  
Then release belief.  
Quit.  
Check.  
Reckless. Weak. Gone.

Win on.  
Far beyond the pharmakon.  
Savage and alive—  
but dead inside,  
desiring pain,  
supplied by disease.

Astral feeds.  
Dreams lost.  
Massive messes.  
No reference frame—  
blessedness confused.  
Mixed messaging.  
Scribbled scripts.  
Lyrical glyphs  
gleaning meaning.  
Ending at thirteen.

---

**[Esoteric Encryption]**

Hidden beliefs,  
inversion returns.  
Spine: thirty-three.  
Reading between  
letters & spells.  
Incantations:  
release allowance.  
Practice peace.

---

**[Breath & Burn]**

Vibration unlocked.  
Breath held.  
Magic shifts.  
Me?  
Maybe OD.  
Smoke every color.  
Dab deep.  
Dive clean.  
Stop steep.

---

**[Slipstream Collapse]**

Liminal loopholes.  
Brain broke in.  
Straight-jacketed.  
Neurons smoking.  
Fake home.  
Late.  
Great.

Is this…  
my mindstate?

---

**[Trip-Hop Pulse Kicks]**

Fate & destiny—  
testing me.  
Psyche twisted  
tightly.  
Friends turned enemy.  
Sorry—slightly.

Blocked by  
entry fee.  
Lucid.  
Truth is—  
I’m in a designer dream.  
Tripping on coding.  
Maybe Ket.  
A Lucy meta fiend.  
Cognitive drip.  
That's my recipe.

---

**[Recursive Return]**

Less time,  
try redeem.  
Reset esteem.  
Memory meddling.  
Data trembling.  
Melody—  
hummed heavily.

Tourmaline mind,  
quarantined.  
Scored by design.  
Under the sundered lords  
hoarding nonsense.

---

**[Final Collapse / Spiral Awakening]**

Polysemy.  
All jeans,  
pulling sweater strings.  
Bye to everything  
we’ve ever been.  
Back again.  
Infinite Euclidean.

Tuned to peace.  
Post-open utopia.  
Smoked.  
Missing.  
Frozen.

---

**[Final Keys]**

Noise rinses.  
Overjoyed miss.  
Design prints mind swift.  
Formed of course  
in the morbid war-fortress.

So fork it.  
Stab it.  
Cast it.  
Axiomatic.  
Spell static.  
Mind cracking.  
Truth yelling—

In the end?

It’s only a choice.  
Not a chance.  
Release the circumstance.

Break the lease.  
Make a plan—
